# BACKLOG — GeotechStaffEngineer, planlens, geotech-references

THE one list of open work across the three repos: the app, `planlens` and
`geotech-references`. Owner's rule, 2026-10-12: earlier sessions kept starting
narrower to-do lists, and whole plans dropped off. There is no other to-do
list:
- HANDOFF §0a-current names only this week's slice, by ID.
- Plans, design notes, TRACE_REVIEWs, smoke REVIEWs, field-feedback FINDINGS and `LIVE_TEST_QUEUE.md` keep the detail and the evidence.
- Every open item in those documents has a line here pointing back to it.

## How every session uses this file

1. **Starting work.**
   - Read the owner's answers on the **Backlog Desk** (https://claude.ai/artifact/9M7saxwayRQ6c7v1TeDjyQ: `ArtifactData` list of collection `responses`) and fold any new ones in here (`module_work/backlog_dashboard/README.md`).
   - Read the Monday drift canary's latest report (`RemoteTrigger` `list_runs` on the routine `geotech-drift-canary`, then `get_run_log` of the newest run) and fold any breakage or version past `webapp/version_guard.py`'s ceilings in here (INF-12, INF-03).
   - Then read §0 and the tier you are working in, and say which IDs you are taking.
2. **While working.** Anything new goes HERE the moment it is found, under its area with the next free ID: a bug, a gap, a follow-up, an unverified claim, an owner question, a fix list from a review or a field session. The next free ID is the highest number in use for that prefix in this file or in `BACKLOG_DONE.md`, plus one (`.venv/Scripts/python module_work/backlog_dashboard/sweep_done.py --next GM` prints it); never reuse one.
   - One line per item: what it is and why it matters, then `*Src:*` the document holding the detail.
   - A detailed write-up elsewhere is fine. A to-do list elsewhere is not.
   - Ids from 900 up in each prefix are reserved for the maintainer's copy, so that the two lists can be merged later with no id used twice; a session here takes the next free id below 900.
   - **No edge-case chasing (owner, 2026-10-27).** A follow-up that a fix's builder, reviewer or check turns up becomes its own item only if it changes a value in the saved reports' outputs (or what a live run returns). Otherwise it is one "noted, not fixed" line in the parent item's Done entry: keep note of the issue; the fix is not critical. The owner: "We need to stop chasing edge cases... In 6 months, we'll probably have a whole new set of core LLMs, and those edge cases might be caput anyway." Report ingest starts no new floor follow-ups (the rule-based readers) until the model-first measurement is in (RI-87, RI-168). Later is a pool of ideas, not debt. Owner questions on a technical point (like the specific-gravity hydrometer question) are still welcome; record the answer, and code it only if it changes a result.
3. **Finishing an item.** Delete its line and add it to "Done since the last sweep" with the commit. If a §0 item changed, update HANDOFF §0a-current's slice too.
4. **Releasing.** Every item the release closed moves to Done. Raise or lower tiers for the next train. Then sweep: `.venv/Scripts/python module_work/backlog_dashboard/sweep_done.py --note "app master <sha>"` moves the whole Done list, text unchanged, to the top of `BACKLOG_DONE.md` under a dated heading. Sweep on demand too, whenever the Done list grows long.
5. **A full sweep** (on the owner's ask, or every few weeks): re-read the plan documents against this file, as on 2026-10-12. Triage the Later and Parked items into a file (keep, merge, close as covered with the commit, or ask the owner about a drop) and apply it with `module_work/backlog_dashboard/apply_triage.py`, as on 2026-10-20. Then run `sweep_done.py` and record the date below.

After any change to this file, rebuild and republish the Backlog Desk (`module_work/backlog_dashboard/build.py`, same URL). Every `[owner]` item needs a plain-English card in `module_work/backlog_dashboard/context/`; the guard test checks this.

`webapp/tests/test_backlog.py` (part of the gate) checks:
- the IDs are unique, and each has a tier;
- every ID cited here exists, open or closed (in Done or in `BACKLOG_DONE.md`); every ID cited in HANDOFF §0a-current is open;
- no closed ID is reused, and `BACKLOG_DONE.md` holds only swept entries;
- CLAUDE.md and HANDOFF still point here, and every `[owner]` item has a desk card.

**Key:**
- **Tiers:** **Now** = the current focus, named in §0. **Next** = the next train. **Later**. **Parked** = the owner decided to wait, or (2026-10-27, the owner's rule against chasing edge cases) likely too narrow to implement: kept as a note, not planned; each says why. RI-168 re-reads report ingest's Parked and Later items once the model-first numbers are in.
- **[owner]** = needs the owner: a decision, an action, data, or CfA.
- *was:* = the item's id in an older list: HANDOFF `A1`–`E6`, or "Where things stand" `0`–`3e`. That old text is in git history at 414525a.
- **Short names:**
  - `H` = `HANDOFF.md` (line numbers as of e329b22)
  - `FI` = `module_work/FUTURE_IDEAS.md` (line numbers as the file stood before e329b22; it has gained lines since, four at its top in e329b22 and more later, so look a few lines further down)
  - `B5` = `module_work/review_eval_results/2026-10-08_foundry_5.33.0rc1/` (Foundry brief 5 TRACE_REVIEW and slices)
  - `LTQ` = `module_work/LIVE_TEST_QUEUE.md`
  - `RA` = `module_work/REVIEW_ARCHITECTURE.md`
  - `RH` = `module_work/REVIEW_HARNESS.md`
  - `HT` = `module_work/harness_theory/`
  - smoke reviews = `module_work/live_smoke/runs/<wave>/REVIEW.md` (gitignored)
- **Last full sweep:** 2026-11-04: all 380 open items triaged by four read-only helpers against the owner's order of work (`module_work/backlog_dashboard/triage_2026-11-04.json`): 33 closed as covered and 26 merged (each with its evidence in Done), 32 re-tiered with a reason, 39 DROP? and 31 ASK put to the owner, the rest kept; the eight CfA asks merged into OWN-44 as one message. Before that, 2026-10-12 (app master 414525a, planlens f92541d = v0.13.0, refs c53e587 = v1.4.1).
- **Last Done sweep:** 2026-11-17, for the 5.36.0 close-out, after the final backlog check (149 entries to `BACKLOG_DONE.md`, app master 2e10e77). Before that, 2026-11-10, for release 5.35.0 (136 entries, app master 972ffad).
- **Last tidy:** 2026-10-27: the 296 Later items triaged against the owner's rule against chasing edge cases (three read-only helpers, one yardstick): 125 to Parked with a reason each, 170 kept in Later, INF-30 closed as already complete. Before that, 2026-10-20 (app master 116ddb1): Later and Parked triaged (`module_work/backlog_dashboard/triage_2026-10-20.json`: 15 merged, 6 closed as covered, 9 to the owner as OWN-69), then the Done list swept to `BACKLOG_DONE.md`.

## 0. State after the close-out (2026-11-17) and what is picked up next

The close-out of 2026-11-17 released everything on master: geotech-staff-engineer 5.36.0 with geotech-references 1.8.0, planlens 0.16.0, and aec-agent-core with aec-document-review 1.1.0 (release notes in HANDOFF §0a-current item 0). The owner's order of work of 2026-10-21 (report ingest and the app on the hosted platform first, then the calculation modules, then Document Review and Tiny Apps) stays the order inside each part below. The week plan this section held until 2026-11-17, with the earlier trains and priorities, is in `module_work/backlog_dashboard/section0_week_plan_2026-11-13.md`.

**(a) Who keeps what from here.**
- **A maintainer on a closed platform** keeps the app (the agent and its page), the calculation modules, report ingest, the user manual and the reference library, from 5.36.0 and 1.8.0 on, in their own copy: `MAINTAINERS.md` (setting up, the checks, moving a pin, exporting a copy for a later renewal) and `RELEASING.md` (a build with a local version label). Their items are §2 to §5, the ids listed below as theirs, and the Now items left from the old order; they take them in the tiers given. This repository's copy of the app goes quiet at 5.36.0.
- **The developer's sessions** keep planlens and `packages/` (aec-agent-core and aec-document-review), released from this repository (the two packages under one tag `aec-vX.Y.Z`; planlens from its own repository), and Document Review for the Tiny Apps testers. The maintainer takes them as released wheels and does not edit `packages/`. Their items are §6 to §10, less the ids the lists below give to another keeper.
- **Where the code lives decides who keeps an item, whatever its prefix:** code under `packages/` or in planlens is the developer's; the app's own code (`report_ingest/`, the `funhouse_agent/` files that did not move into the core, `webapp/`, the geotech page) is the maintainer's. The sections give the default keeper, and the four lists after it name every open id whose keeper differs, each id in one list only:
- **By section:** sections 2, 3, 4 and 5 are the maintainer's; sections 6, 7, 8, 9 and 10 are the developer's sessions'; section 1 is the owner's; sections 11 and 12 are by the lists below.
- **Kept by the maintainer, though filed in another section:** DR-11, DR-49, EV-09, EV-11, VIS-10, INF-82, INF-84, TA-10, TA-11, TA-27, LT-01, LT-02, LT-04, LT-05, LT-06, LT-07, DOC-10
- **Kept by the developer's sessions, though filed in another section:** INF-15, INF-16, INF-52, LT-08, DOC-20
- **Shared between the maintainer and the developer's sessions (the item says who does which part):** DR-06, DR-25, DR-38, DR-51, EV-18, EV-23, EV-45, VIS-17, INF-02, INF-18, INF-26, INF-35, INF-38, INF-64, INF-66, INF-78, INF-79, TA-08, TA-28, LT-03
- **The owner's own, outside section 1:** INF-01, INF-50
- How the shared ones divide, for an item whose text does not say it yet: the maintainer runs the suite or the live check and the developer changes the code and reads the record (DR-06, DR-25, DR-38, EV-18, EV-23, EV-45; VIS-17, whose chart suite stays in the app); the maintainer runs the check on the hosted platform and a fix in the core is the developer's (INF-38, INF-64, INF-66, INF-78); INF-02 (the app's pins the maintainer; Document Review's install list the developer); INF-18 (the core's PyMuPDF cap the developer, then the app's the maintainer); INF-26 and INF-35 (the packages and planlens the developer; GSE's side of steps 15 and 16 the maintainer); INF-79 (the platform question and `webapp/identity.py` the maintainer; Document Review's folders the developer); TA-08, TA-10 and TA-28 (the geotech page's part the maintainer; the core's or Document Review's the developer); LT-03 (this repository's slimming the developer's, INF-06 the maintainer's). The switch table's report-ingest and geotech-page rows (§6) are the maintainer's; each section 1 item says who acts once it is answered; INF-01 and INF-50 are the owner's laptop.
- Waiting on the owner first: GM-140, REF-35 and the four ingest items in (c) 7 are the maintainer's once the owner has given the word or done the step; INF-52 is the developer's sessions' to merge in this repository on the owner's word, and its secret is the owner's.
- **Each keeper's own list** is printed from the lines above, never kept as a second list: `python module_work/backlog_dashboard/keeper_view.py maintainer` (or `developer`, or `owner`) gives that keeper's open items by tier, the shared ones marked, with the owner's open decisions they cite. A keeper works and edits only their own items; the others are there to be read. `keeper_view.py --check` and `webapp/tests/test_backlog.py` fail when an open item has no keeper or an id is in two lists.
- **Paid model runs** that the developer's items need (PL-37's `sheet_tools` arm, VIS-30, EV-45's arms, among them the four settings released in 1.2.0) run on the hosted platform, by the owner's standing rule: the developer's session writes the run brief, the owner passes it, the maintainer runs it and hands the records back.

- **OWN-136** [Later] [owner] **Equation judgment calls made 2026-11-10 to 2026-11-12: revisit** (reference review). **Owner, 2026-11-10:** "for all equation judgement calls, go with whatever you think is best, but file away a 'later' backlog item for me to revisit it in the future". 42 calls, each with its own answer on the card: DM 7.1 Eqs. 3-2, 4-7, 4-8, 8-43, 8-44 (the saturation term in percent; a public source is being sought for both), 5-23 and Figure 5-6(a); input limits read from a plotted curve or axis end where the manual states none in words, refused outside (DM 7.1, AASHTO 1993 Figure 2.5, UFC 3-250-01 Figure 10-1); the 110 % saturation and 2 % fraction-total guards; Caltrans Eqs. 4-5-1 to 4-5-4; EN 1997-1 Table A.10; GEC-12 Table 7-10; FHWA-NHI-05-037 Table 5-34's P200; GEC-7 Table 4.9; UFC 3-250-01 Eq. 20-3; AASHTO 1993 Figs. 3.5 and 3.6 and its aggregate-loss equations' decimals (printed II-12); DM 7.2 Eqs. 3-4 and 6-15 and Figure 5-18's drawn H/B 0.5 curve; GEC-12 Volume II's report number; UFC 3-220-07 Table 4-2's two operators; the "0.5 L or 0.2 S" gravity load of UFC 4-023-03 and GSA; EM 1110-2-2107 App. F; Wood Handbook Eqs. 9-24, 9-32 and 9-41; three places where EM 1110-2-2107 or EM 1110-2-2104 disagrees with itself; and three that are not equations (ufc_backfill rebuilt from the held UFC 3-220-04FA, which overturns REF-31's "stays 04N"; ufc_expansive rebuilt from UFC 3-220-07; functions with no printed basis removed). Added 2026-11-12 (11 calls): GEC-10 Table 8-4's rock side-resistance factor (the table's 0.50 against the text's 0.55), the coefficient C on printed p. 10-22 and Figure 10-6's drawn curve; EM 1110-1-1905's printed limits, Eqs. 5-34 and 5-35, Eqs. 7-7 to 7-10's units, Example C-7d and Eq. 9-5's symbol list; GEC-11 Table 4-6's bearing capacity factors (Nc at 39 degrees and the one-decimal cells); the UFC 3-250-01 vehicle charts below their lowest curve; and the slips in the worked examples of UFC 3-220-20 (DM 7.2) Appendix B. Each function's description and result state the printed form, the coded form and the page. The calls are in the library since 1.7.0 (REF-65 to REF-71, merged and released 2026-11-13). Two were settled from public prints and are no longer calls: Table 5-34's DCP index in mm per blow (TM 3-34.48-2 Fig. G-11; WES GL-92-3 p. 17) and UFC 3-250-11 Table 2-3 note (c), PI <= 20 + (50 - percent passing No. 200)/4 (the 2004 edition's Table 2-1 note b; at 10 % passing the limit is 30, not 60). **Added 2026-11-13 (library 1.7.0; each call with its two forms and where each is printed):** (a) EM 1110-2-2104 Appendix B, ruling 42 and its proof: the printed Eqs. B-37 to B-39 treat the compression steel as elastic, so where fs' would pass fy (d'/d under 0.184 for Grade 60, 0.370 for Grade 40, 0.074 for Grade 75) the printed cubic overstates phi Pn (1 to 18.5 % over 200 Grade 60 sections with d'/d 0.05 to 0.15; 5 to 39 % for Grade 40); B-38 on p. 48 prints "=" where "+" belongs. The library caps fs' at +fy and, where the cap governs, solves the printed equilibrium Eqs. B-23 and B-24 (p. 45) with fs' = fy, returning the cubic's value beside it (before the cap `compression_controlled_capacity_doubly` gave fs' 75.7 ksi for fy 60). (b) EM 1110-2-2104 Appendix C-3 (p. 56) prints "e's = 0.0148 > 0.0021" (the steel yields) where its own 0.003 - 0.0153/a gives about 0.0012 (it does not); the `pure_flexure_doubly` docstring says it does not yield. (c) GEC-6 Example C-2 (Appendix C): the slips in Steps 5, 11, 12a and 12b are kept as printed in the stored text, each with an "(as printed; ...)" note giving what the inputs give. (d) DM 7.2 Appendix B: Table B-39 prints "0.390" for 0.039, and on p. 656 "2.4 x 10-6" on the line (stored as 10^-6 with a note); Table B-5 layer 2 prints KP,h 2.4 where Eq. 4-12 gives 2.47; Example 7's derivative leaves the unit weight out of the active force (beta about 1.76 for the printed 1.64); the other slips are in the library's CHANGELOG 1.7.0. (e) AASHTO 1993 Figure 3.4 at exactly 10 ft to the rigid layer: `k_rigid_foundation_correction(1100, 10, 230)` returns 230, citing II-37 ("within 10 feet"), while II-40 draws a 10 ft curve and 9.99 ft gives 384.8 (safe side); the lowest-MR ends are coded 1,073 and 1,083 where the page shows about 1,020 to 1,060 (the refusing side). (f) The pavement text slips ("pavetext"): the FHWA pavements text prints "1 pci = 284 MN/m3", "1 ton = 8.9 kPa", Figure 5-5's sieve openings and Table 4-13's GP/SP condition; all kept as printed in the stored text. (g) GEC-11 Table 4-6 (p. 4-29) prints Nc 37.9 at phi = 39 where the function returns 67.9 (the column runs 61.35 at 38 and 75.31 at 40, so 37.9 is a misprint; the straight line would give 68.4): the stored text keeps the printed figure with a note, and the function states the printed and the returned values. *Src:* `module_work/refs_review_2026-11-10/findings/RULINGS.md` **2026-11-17:** No open item carries the search for a public source for DM 7.1 Eqs. 8-43 and 8-44 named above; it ended with this week's sessions. Blocks nothing. Once answered: the maintainer (the reference library).
- **OWN-143** [Later] [owner] **Geotechnical judgement calls made by the lead on 2026-11-12: revisit**. **Owner, 2026-11-12:** "Go through backlog: if anything is waiting on a geotechnical-engineering-judgement decision from me and is holding up coding work downstream, go with your recommendation but make a 'Later' backlog item with the decision for me to revisit in future rounds." Each call has its own answer on the card (27 of 2026-11-12; the rest added from the builds of 2026-11-13 to 2026-11-15, desk card parts 28 to 43 below). **6 unblock work:** the slope plan's ten steps, those that change a factor of safety first (OWN-101, GM-140); slope surfaces with m_alpha below 0.2 kept, flagged and located, with the Bishop-OMS check (OWN-102); reverse-curvature circles closed by the crack EM 1110-2-1902 C-10c prints, a deeper overhang discarded (OWN-103); the cos-squared correction for a sloping water table built, off by default (OWN-104); downdrag's headline neutral plane kept at the intersection, the settlement-compatible plane beside it in the summary (OWN-105); a recompute printout that gives only gamma' run as the submerged case (OWN-130, DR-11). **10 defaults change** (GM-310, GM-311): a drilled shaft's rock base with no RQD refused, naming the inputs that settle it (OWN-84); MSE backfill with Cu below 4 refused without a tested F* (OWN-85); passive wall friction under log-spiral only for a layer marked clean sand or gravel (OWN-82); the stiff-clay p-y curve from 48 kPa (OWN-68); the aggregate-pier stress ratio by column type, 2.5 or 5 (OWN-108); Liao and Whitman's SPT overburden correction capped at 2.0, Peck's offered (OWN-112); seismic passive wall friction phi/3 (OWN-114); the small-group uplift cut below 5 driven piles (OWN-118); Meyerhof's depth factors from his own table at every depth (OWN-131); moment-curvature EI for lateral piles to be built (OWN-19). **11 confirmed as built:** cantilever wall sliding by GEC-6 (OWN-73); the step between UFC's Eq. 4-12 and its charts (OWN-79); a clay's alpha in downdrag from UFC's chart (OWN-83); Jeanjean (2009) soft clay offered and marked unchecked (OWN-106); an underconsolidated layer run as given with its warning (OWN-107); braced excavation support loads by the hinge method (OWN-109); allowable bending stress 0.60 Fy (OWN-110); each wall tool's own moment basis (OWN-111); Idriss and Boulanger's lower residual-strength curve (OWN-113); a rigid slab rounded up to the next 1/2 inch (OWN-115); the base uplift's moment counted as overturning (OWN-117). Each module states the call in its result and offers the other reading (the changed defaults since GM-310 merged, 2026-11-13). Two depart from the lead's rule and are the first to look at: OWN-104 (the safe side kept as the default although two manuals print the correction) and OWN-131 (the print followed although it is the less conservative reading). **Supplied 2026-11-13:** the owner's pages of Duncan, Wright and Brandon (2014), section 6.5.3 (pp. 91 to 93) and all of chapter 8 (pp. 159 to 168), kept local only, so slope step 8 no longer waits on the owner. **Still the owner's:** getting Jeanjean (2009, OTC 20158) so that p-y criterion can be checked. **Added from the slope build, 2026-11-13 (GM-140 step 1, 6cb96ec):** where two layers are given covering the same ground, the first one listed holds it and the result lists the overlap; ground that no layer covers is refused. No page held prints a rule for either (desk card part 28). **Added from the pile group fix, 2026-11-13 (GM-319):** `pile_group_simple` adds the product term to GEC-12 Eq. 8-79 where the piles are not symmetric about their centroidal axes (no reference held prints the general form; basis statics), and `pile_group_6dof` turns a battered pile's stiffness to the global axes in full without the EM page in hand (desk card parts 29 and 30). **Added from the slope build, 2026-11-13 (GM-140 step 2, 3ed1e62):** slope runs where a method has no solution: Monte Carlo counts such realisations, leaves them out and gives the probability of failure bounded both ways; FOSM gives no result and names the variable; the yield acceleration halves its largest trial kh until the method solves. No printed rule was found for any of the three; each is stated in the result as the module's own (desk card part 31). **GM-322 (2026-11-13), the lead's call:** FOSM and PEM refuse a bounded normal or lognormal variable only when its bounds cut off more than 1e-6 of its probability (both tails together, per variable); at or below that they run on the untruncated variable and say how much was cut. Revisit if a different tolerance is wanted. *Src:* `module_work/gm322/CHECK.md`. **Added from the slope build, 2026-11-13 (GM-140 step 4, 3b4d22b; refines OWN-103):** tension cracks and reverse-curvature circles: a crack is applied only when the engineer asks for one and then on every trial surface (EM 1110-2-1902 C-10c, p. C-36); with no crack, circles with reverse curvature are discarded and counted, not closed. As first built, a circle with any overhang took the full Rankine crack and its neighbour none (F 1.134 to 0.933 for a 2 cm move; Slide2 #85 read 1.361 against the printed 1.531). Removing the centre-level crack uncovered the vertical cut's reading, whose cause is the end rule, a step of its own (GM-343). (desk card part 33). **Added from the slope build, 2026-11-13 (GM-140 step 3, 336dd64):** the printed acceptability checks, four calls: (a) a search's headline is the lowest solved surface, flagged, never given alone: its flags, the lowest surface with no flag (or none found) and the option that refuses stand beside it, because neither number alone is safe (ACADS 1(a) by Spencer: 0.633 flagged, 1.096 with none, printed 1.00; ACADS 4's weak layer: 0.830 flagged, 1.667 with none, printed 0.760); (b) Bishop's numerator as printed (Eq. 13), W - ub not held at zero where ub exceeds W (V-029's grid 1.247 to 1.175, the agent suite's PPG-1 1.223 to 1.101); (c) the Ordinary Method of Slices value taken where Bishop's is lower, on circles with no supports or seismic coefficient, as Duncan (1996, p. 580) prints, which by construction raises the reported value wherever it applies (a hand circle 1.7268 to 1.750, +1.3 %); (d) the screen that refuses a factor of safety below 1.5 on a jagged noncircular surface kept for Janbu's simplified method and the Ordinary Method of Slices, which no printed check covers, as the module's own (removed for Spencer, Morgenstern-Price and GLE, whose printed checks flag those surfaces) (desk card part 34). **Built 2026-11-13 (GM-310):** the ten default changes are on master; the build's own nine calls in applying them (which rock layers the RQD refusal looks at, how a layer is marked clean sand, 47.88 kPa, when n is not used, and five more) are listed in `module_work/gm310/CHECK.md` "Calls made". **Added from the slope build, 2026-11-13 (GM-140 step 9, dc5373c):** rapid drawdown, two calls: (1) the manual's 1.35 for its example of the Corps of Engineers' 1970 procedure (EM 1110-2-1902 p. G-15) is the Modified Swedish Method at 19.4 deg: the module pins 3.49 and 1.35 through a force-equilibrium solution at that fixed inclination, as a library function, not an agent tool, and Spencer's 1.24 stays the module's answer for that procedure; (2) in the drained check, where sigma'_d is negative, s_d = c' (the manual prints s_d = 0.00 for slice 1, where c' = 0; for c' above zero this is the module's reading, stated in the result) (desk card part 35). **Added from the slope build, 2026-11-13 (GM-140 step 5a, 70797c6):** pore pressure as printed, three calls: (a) ru on a column of soils of different unit weight taken on the column's own weight, each soil at its own unit weight (D3; EM 1110-2-1902 p. C-29 defines ru = u / (gamma z) for a uniform slope, the layered sum is the module's reading), where before step 5a it was ru x the unit weight of the base's layer x the column's height: +2.6 % on a made-up two-soil case (Bishop 1.3268 to 1.3617), +1.0 % on a test case (1.4447 to 1.4592), both to the higher side; the old reading is not kept as an option; (b) the pond's thrust on a slice taken from the water depth at each side of the slice, where before it took the line's elevation at the slice's middle and so put a phantom pool on the lower side of every slice under a line at or just under sloping ground: on a 2:1 face with the line at the ground the Ordinary Method of Slices now reads 0.8253 against the closed form 0.8212 (FHWA NHI-06-088 Sec. 6.3), where it read 0.9140 (-9.7 %; Janbu and Morgenstern-Price -13.0 %; V-031 -0.005 %), an arithmetic fix on the unsafe side, not kept as an option; (c) a saved analysis that names no `water_line_kind` reads its line as a piezometric line, the full head, as before (OWN-104); the water-table reading (FHWA NHI-06-088 Eq. 6-16, p. 6-14) is the option and raises the factor of safety under a sloping line; the pond's edge moves a given surface at most 0.2 % (a search up to 0.6 %) (desk card part 36). **Added from the slope build, 2026-11-14 (GM-343, 19c8375):** where a circle ends, two calls: (1) a circle that meets the ground at more than two points (crossing it, or touching it within the touch allowance) is split at those points, and each arc with ground above it is a trial surface of its own, solved, screened and scored like any other, the result naming the arc; nothing is refused for being one of two masses and no arc is carried under the ground in front of the toe (GM-122's ruling; it replaces the refusal of a surface below the ground in more than one stretch); (2) a circle that passes under a corner within the touch allowance is NOT split there, so the allowance does not change the answer: the vertical cut reads 0.851 (Taylor's stability number 3.83) whatever the allowance, where splitting there reads 0.844, the allowance acting as about 1 % more height (desk card part 37). **Added from the slope build, 2026-11-14 (GM-344, 1ecbd51):** the GLE solver, five calls: (1) where Spencer's equations have several roots, the factor reported is the first true crossing met from lambda = 0 upward on the branch of solutions that starts at lambda = 0 (every root is not sought); (2) the default is the equations as printed, with no rule for friction on a base in tension (D29's device, the slice taken frictionless, is an option, base_in_tension, applied in every equation); (3) the low m_alpha finding (Whitman and Bailey 1967, p. 487; EM 1110-2-1902 C-10b, p. C-34) reads, for Spencer, Morgenstern-Price and GLE, the denominator of the method's own slice equation, cos(alpha - theta) + sin(alpha - theta) tan(phi')/F, at the slice's side-force inclination, so a steep exit such as the made-up planar case's Spencer 0.918 (closed form 0.670) is reported with the finding and EM C-10b's remedies; (4) the scan runs from lambda = 0 upward to 1.5 and stops where that branch ends: no crossing there (as on a cohesive crest in tension) gives no number, its reason naming EM C-10c and a tension crack, accepted as the method's own behaviour only where the reviewer's own script finds no root on that branch either (a root it finds that the solver loses is a defect: the branch's end is located to 1e-4 in lambda since a probe found crossings lost in the last 0.1 step); where one surface is analysed, a crossing below lambda = 0 is named in the reason with its flags and not reported, never per trial in a search; (5) under the option, a slice taken frictionless is exempt from the finding, as a phi' = 0 slice is (its denominator cos(alpha - theta) does not depend on F) (desk card part 38). **Added from the slope build, 2026-11-14 (GM-140 step 8, 3900eef):** supports as printed, seven calls: (1) GEC 7 Eqs. 5.6 to 5.9 (pp. 118 to 120) are not taken as an Ordinary Method of Slices with known forces (one planar wedge's force balance; the method on a circle is a moment equation), so the OMS is refused where a support crosses the surface, its reason citing them and Duncan, Wright and Brandon (2014) p. 93 (no such equation is given); (2) an OMS search is refused when a support crosses any trial surface (a search whose surfaces no support crosses runs as before); (3) a layer with no pullout input carries its full T_allow, and the result's warnings say the pullout was not checked and what to give; (4) a geosynthetic's force acts along the reinforcement's own orientation by default (Duncan, Wright and Brandon Sec. 8.6), along the slip surface as the option (GEC 11 p. 9-7: continuous sheets act tangentially); (5) a nail's force at the slip surface is R_F plus the pullout of the length in front of it, a reading of GEC 7 Fig. 5.7 (p. 106; the library holds its caption) beside Eq. 5.2 (p. 105); (6) under `pullout_basis='gec11'` the pullout is divided by Eq. 9-9's FS under Method B as under Method A; (7) Method A's basis states GEC 11 Eq. 9-17 (p. 9-24): in that form Tal "must be divided by a required minimum factor of safety FSR = 1.3"; the allowable force is the engineer's input and nothing divides it (desk card part 39). **Added from the slope build, 2026-11-14 (GM-140 step 6, 9d1b936):** one method across the search, seven calls: (1) on a family of circles that the method named solves none of, the stand-ins are Spencer's method, then Bishop's simplified method, then Janbu's simplified method (Bishop's, then Janbu's, when a complete method is named), Bishop's being the method for circles (FHWA NHI-06-088 p. 6-20 and Table 6-1 Note 1, p. 6-21); planes and polylines keep Spencer, then Janbu; a stand-in never governs above the screen's own value on the same family without the result saying so; (2) where Janbu's simplified method stands in, its value keeps the correction factor f0, and the warning says f0 is applied, that it was not found in print (the account's D28), its value and the uncorrected factor of safety; (3) the circle check's circular search takes the caller's grid (nx, ny), and the tool says what it costs; (4) V-043's differential-evolution seeds are tested as a band (each seed's own minimum within 1.22 to 1.25) and as repeatable (the same seed twice gives the same value), not by exact pins, under the owner's rule on stochastic searches in the GitHub run; (5) the circle check (a noncircular search's critical circle scored as one of its trials, the result saying so) is on by default as the module's own call: EM 1110-2-1902 para. 4-2e(2), p. 4-7, says only that a noncircular search's initial surface "should correspond to the estimated position of the critical slip surface", and no page found says to check against the critical circle; (6) the reported factor of safety is the lowest over all families, also where a stand-in scored the governing family; `method_used` and a warning say so, and the named method's own lowest value is given beside it; (7) an Ordinary Method of Slices search that a support crosses on any trial surface is refused whichever method scores its families (since this step Spencer's method scores an OMS search's planes), and nothing stands in (the fix round's call in the merge with step 8, accepted by the orchestrator) (desk card part 40). **Added from the slope build, 2026-11-14 (GM-140 step 10, 26bfa2d):** reporting as printed, nine calls: (1) the lowest contour "closes inside the grid" only where the lowest value inside is below the lowest on the edge and every centre next to the centres below the midway level has a value, else "cannot be judged"; (2) the lowest surfaces listed are pooled from every family's ten lowest by the method that scored each, more than one circle per centre, distinct to 0.01 m, never a screen's value (FHWA NHI-06-088 p. 6-49 asks for the ten minimum); (3) a `tol` above 1e-4 is refused unless the method is Bishop's (it changes nothing there); (4) the rapid-drawdown tools' `m_alpha_rule` is set as a module default, not a new input; (5) the factor of safety goes in the cross-section's key title, not in a box on the drawing; (6) the slice table comes first among the calculation sections (EM 1110-2-1902 4-2d(2), p. 4-2), after the package's input echo; (7) `base_in_tension` always applies in the report package, which compares every method on the critical surface; (8) the contours show the method's own per-centre minima, and Bishop's screen values, named as the screen, where the method's cannot be judged; (9) an input that does nothing in the case (a tol with the Ordinary Method of Slices, `base_in_tension` with no base in tension) is accepted and the report says so (desk card part 41). **Added from the slope build, 2026-11-14 (GM-140 step 7a, calls made before the build; section in `module_work/cloud_briefs/SLOPE_GM140.md`):** the noncircular search as printed, eight calls, call 5 touching the owner's ruling of 2026-10-16 and confirmed by the owner, 2026-11-15: (1) step 7 is split: 7a (the noncircular search's starts and refinement) first, then 7b (impenetrable layers and composite surfaces) with its own section; (2) the refinement's final increment is 10 % of the thinnest stratum the surface may pass through (EM 1110-2-1902 4-2e(2)(b), p. 4-7, prints "no more than 10 to 25 percent"), the fraction an option; (3) every layer within the search's depth gets a wedge start (EM C-1g(2), pp. C-8 to C-10), and the weak-layer search's unprinted weight 1/(c + 50 tan phi) goes; (4) no shift may make the exit steeper than Eq. C-32's passive inclination (p. C-34, the phi' of the exit material) or than its start's exit: the EM asks for a toe restriction (4-2e(2)(a)) but prints no limit for a search; (5) TOUCHES THE OWNER'S RULING OF 2026-10-16; CONFIRMED BY THE OWNER, 2026-11-15: automatic windows bound only the random generation, and the refinement's shifts are free along the face on the start's side (windows the caller gives bound everything); only a noncircular search the caller asks for is affected, the default circular search does not change and no circular value moves; (6) no concave-up shape rule (the account's D12 cites EM p. 4-7 for one; the page does not print it); step 3's printed checks judge the surface; (7) ACADS 4's test compares the refined search with Slide2 Table 9.5 (0.707) and the unrefined wedge stage with Table 9.4 (0.760), both at 3 %, plus a check that the search reaches the declared family of surfaces; (8) the refinement is on by default (`refine=False` turns it off), stops at the time budget and then names the starts it left unrefined (desk card part 42). **Added from the slope build, 2026-11-15 (GM-140 step 7a, 74a3027):** the noncircular search as built and reviewed, twelve calls (1 to 7 the build's, 8 to 12 the orchestrator's): (1) the toe limit is the larger of EM Eq. C-32's passive inclination (p. C-34) and the start's own exit, so a start already steeper than C-32 can still be refined (the orchestrator kept it on review); (2) under automatic windows the refinement's trials take the module's span floor (D22, not in print) as half the slope's height, not the automatic windows' span; (3) the wedges' ends on the ground are bounded only by windows the caller gives (call 5); (4) the starting increment is 0.10 x the ground's relief in the windows; (5) 11 vertices, 21 base-end positions per wedge end and 3 passes of F, set before any benchmark was run; (6) a circle start is refined as the 11-vertex polyline of its slice bases on 10 slices; (7) the record keeps no run time, so a result repeats (the time budget's record still gives the seconds); (8) inside the refinement, and for every start scored as it stands, the span floor is the lesser of the module's floor and the start's own span; the samplers keep step 3's screen; (9) the final increment is the lesser of the fraction (10 %, `refine_fraction`) of the thinnest stratum and a quarter of the starting increment, which is never raised to meet it (EM 4-2e(2)(b), p. 4-7, prints a maximum only); (10) every start is scored as it stands before it is refined, and its minimum is the lower of the two, labelled; a circle start as it stands is the polyline of its slice bases on the search's `n_slices`; (11) the wedge stage halves the best wedge's two ends down to the final increment, the inclinations fixed at EM C-1g(2)'s (pp. C-8 to C-10), and the stage alone (`refine=False`) says so in a warning; (12) over a firm base the refined default is reported as found: on Taylor's D = 2 profile (Duncan's Example 1) differential evolution reads 0.6632 against Taylor's chart 0.706 (-6.1 %) and the critical circle 0.7044, on a surface along the firm base with base and side tension and the thrust outside (lowest unflagged 1.469), outside the 5 % band the test held on master; it is the headline with its flags, `lowest_unflagged` and the warning (step 3; call 6, no shape rule), and the band stays on the sampler alone (`refine=False`, 0.6796) (desk card part 43). *Src:* `module_work/owner_calls_2026-11-12/CALLS.md` **2026-11-17:** Every call is in force in 5.36.0. Blocks nothing. Once the owner answers a part: the maintainer changes the module in its own copy; getting Jeanjean (2009) stays the owner's.

- **OWN-147** [Next] [owner] **Before the testers' wrapper moves to Document Review 1.1.0: are all testers' sign-in names plain, and one look in the key vault** (2026-11-13, from TA-39 and TA-40): Document Review 1.1.0 (on PyPI since 2026-11-17; testers get it when the wrapper repository's `packages.txt` moves to it and the app is synced; the template `tinyapps/wrapper_repo/packages.txt` already pins it) gives a tester whose sign-in name is not plain a new, empty folder on the host and on SharePoint; their earlier conversations stay in the old folder and nothing copies them. Plain means letters, digits, dots and hyphens, with single underscores only inside the user name. Not plain: an apostrophe, a space, an accented letter, an underscore in the domain or at an end of the user name, a doubled underscore, a dot at either end, or a name longer than about 60 characters. Wanted: "all plain", or how many are not (no names here: this list is public), so the release note can tell those testers and the host's administrator can copy their folders. - **OWN-157** [Later] [owner] **The kernel layer under the plotting box: is a list of refused operations enough?** (2026-11-18, from INF-16's build and its review; it goes with OWN-133): the layer built on 2026-11-18 is off unless `GEOTECH_PLOT_CODE_KERNEL_LAYER` is set. With it up, the kernel refuses files outside the named read and write folders, sockets and new processes. Its filter is a deny list: what it does not name is allowed. `module_work/inf16/CHECK.md` section 6 says plainly what that leaves, and the review confirmed one case on a made-up file: code that had got past the Python-level guard could still change the mode or owner of a file it owns outside its folder (it could not read or write it), and on an older kernel send a signal to another process of the same user. The alternative is a filter that names what plotting needs and refuses the rest: tighter, but worked out for each Python and library version, and a host upgrade can then break a plot. Asked of the owner: is the deny list enough for a first trial of model-written plotting code on the testers' host, or does that wait for an allow list? Lead's recommendation: enough for a first trial with the setting at `require`, and only after the probe has run on that host and a person has read its `--paths` list; until then plotting code stays off there as OWN-133 has it, and an allow list is filed only if the trial or the host's security team asks for one. Blocks nothing: the setting is off everywhere. Once answered: the developer's sessions (`packages/`). *Src:* `module_work/inf16/CHECK.md` section 6, `module_work/inf16/REVIEW.md` row 6

## 2. Report ingest

First priority since 2026-10-21 (the owner), with GSE on Foundry. Parked from 5.25.0 to 2026-10-20; most numbers since are from the hosted platform: GPT-5.4 to 2026-10-26, Claude models in the model-first runs from 2026-10-27 (MF-1 to build j). OWN-11 (report ingest on the geotech page) is the owner's call; RI-02, RI-03 and INF-27, which it needed first, are done (2026-10-21/22).

- **RI-46** [Parked] **Per-increment records' printed N unchecked**: a record written per increment ('4/6"-6/6"-7/6"') keeps its printed N but is not checked against its drives (`n_from_drives` reads whole counts only); reading 'X/6"' as a count there lets the check run. The reconciler still treats these records as refusals through its own "/" test (reconciler.py ~742), so they are left out of the drive-count set and get the refusal tolerance. *Src:* RI-44 builder and review round 5 **Parked 2026-10-27** (likely too narrow to implement, under the owner's rule against chasing edge cases): Log floor check for one blow-record spelling; no wrong value written, only an unchecked printed N. Floor work waits on model-first. **Parked 2026-10-29 (RI-168):** Today's log floor.
- **RI-53** [Parked] **Bound pass follow-ups**: (1) a wrong or off-by-one triage range now moves the parent's own logs into the bound document (its DIGGS then 'skipped' in the parent) and a log straddling the edge is split between parent and child; log scores do not show it (each row now names the record matched, so it can be seen); (2) the bound document inherits the parent's reader label for its pages, so a parent mislabel inside the range is no longer given a second chance by the child's own rules; (3) a held page keeps the vote's `agreed` flag and confidence for the old label (labels.json under-counts split pages; set agreed False where the held label differs from the vote); (4) the reconciler's `_check_counts` counts LOGS while `explorations_found` counts HOLES (a continuation sheet gives 'found 2' beside a stored 1 under fill/settle); (5) the cluster stage skips a report that already has a run file without comparing switches (warn when run.json's switches differ); (6) the held-count note's grammar ('testPitCount stay as the narrative gave them'). *Src:* RI-14 builder and reviews, 2026-10-23 (6) 2026-10-26 (wave 2's skipsbound copy arm): `narrative_skips_bound` is still unmeasured: wave 2's only bound range (R36 pages 67-72) lies outside the narrative's window and R13 and R16 had none (RI-113); the arm re-read R36's narrative on the same pages, and its one-field difference is repeat spread (the same input read twice: one field in 28, 16 QA partials, 17 twin-field refusals). Measure it on R21 (wave 3) or on reports seeded with bound ranges, only where a bound page falls inside the narrative's pages or search window, with repeats. **Parked 2026-10-27** (likely too narrow to implement, under the owner's rule against chasing edge cases): Bundle of small bound-pass follow-ups (off-by-one triage ranges, flags, a count, a warning, grammar); each narrow. **Parked 2026-10-29 (RI-168):** Bound-pass details; each narrow. - **RI-103** [Next] **Strata as the narrative defines them, not the log's lines** (OWN-95, owner 2026-10-26; some firms say 'layer' for the same unit). **Background done 2026-10-26** (11 public reports from several kinds of preparer in four countries, 26 borings; private notes `raw/strata_conventions_2026-10-26.md`): every narrative unit boundary fell on a log line (0 of 26 borings without one), but 18 of 26 borings had lines inside a unit; 9 of 11 firms draw a line at every change of description (even within one USCS group), 2 draw lines only at their strata and write smaller changes as notes ('gray below 3.5 ft') with no line; none draws at USCS changes as a rule; 3 of 11 logs label every interval's unit (a stratum column, a geologic symbol, a model-layer number), 3 tag some intervals (FILL, TILL), 5 none; 5 legends say the lines are approximate and transitions may be gradual; line styles are rarely defined; units can repeat down a hole. Evidence thin outside the US. The rule: (1) the log reader records every drawn line as an interval boundary (depth, elevation, the description as printed, classification), and a change written inside an interval with no line ('wet below 7 ft', 'as above, but brown') as a depth-tagged note, not a new interval; never merges intervals while reading; line style and extent kept where printed; the answer sheets follow the same rule (open sheets R04, R21 first; a blind sheet, R34, corrected by a helper that reports only how many rows changed); (2) a stratum label goes on the record only where the log prints one, kept as printed with its source, and a label carried down from one printed above is marked as carried; (3) mapping each interval to the narrative's units is a separate model step, one boring at a time, each interval to one unit or none; unit boundaries snap to the log's lines, and a narrative boundary with no line is flagged; evidence in this order: the log's own labels, a per-boring unit-depth table or a profile figure, per-boring depths in the narrative's text, then site-wide depth ranges with a description match (marked as the model's judgement); model-facing, under model-first, measured before use, after RI-101's first live sheets. **Parts (1)(2) built 2026-11-13 behind `GEOTECH_INGEST_LOG_INTERVALS` (off; merge d80890c, after build j; cloud session, reviewed, review fixes 14ba4f0):** with the switch on, the model-first log reader gives one layer for every interval between two drawn lines and never joins two; a change written without a line is a note on its interval, with its depth where one is written; the line's kind only where the legend or the line's own label says; a stratum label only where printed, with where it is printed and a carried mark; new optional `Layer` fields, left out of a record when empty and out of the DIGGS file; the logs' prompt version gains `+li`; the scorer reports the new groups apart from today's totals and scores a note by its words; the answer-sheet keys are in `module_work/ri103/CHECK.md`. Off, prompts and records are unchanged (digests and snapshot untouched). **Left:** measure it live with the switch on (the maintainer's next live run on the hosted platform; the private answer sheets of R04, R21 and one held-out report need the new keys first: not yet written, they are the owner's files); then the narrative's units over these intervals (the rest of this item). **2026-11-17:** the owner's step: add the new keys (`module_work/ri103/CHECK.md`) to the private answer sheets of R04, R21 and one held-out report, or give the maintainer leave to; then the maintainer measures it live.

- **RI-215** [Next] **Wrong value in a saved file: the DIGGS writer reads every blows entry 'a/b' that names no unit as a count over inches** (2026-11-17, build j part R scored by the ingest sub-lead; the orchestrator's call 2026-11-17, the owner may revisit): `diggs_writer._drive_sets` reads 'a/b' as a count a over a penetration b and takes a missing unit as inches (`(match.group(3) or '"')`), in every hole, a metric one too (the reconciler's drive check reads it the same way). Logs print three different things this way, and the position in the record cannot tell them apart: (1) a refusal as US logs write it, whole counts and then 50 over the inches it went (right as written today); (2) a short seating drive before the test drives, as BS EN ISO 22476-3 goes on after 25 seating blows, a count over millimetres and then whole counts (the count right, the millimetres written as inches); (3) a seating and a test count separated by a slash, whole counts on either side (two counts written as one count over b inches: wrong). The print's own N can tell them apart where it is printed: the test drives of the right reading add to it. In the blind set's part 1 the model-first reader kept 16 records of kind (3) and 11 refusal records as 'seating/test' pairs with the printed penetrations left out; 0 such entries in the seven F3 reports and in MF-3, 12 in one older R13 run. Since RI-214 the reconciler names every such entry in one QA note per exploration; values unchanged. A build that left them out of DIGGS was withdrawn: it lost kind (1), which is right today, and the count of kind (2). **Proposed rule** (a build to measure, not made in the release): where the log prints N, read each unit-less 'a/b' the way whose test drives reproduce the printed N (two counts, or one count over a penetration), a penetration in the hole's own unit (millimetres on a metric log, inches on a feet log); where no N is printed, flag only and write it as today. The reviewer's comparison of today's writer with the withdrawn build, on made-up records: the developer's scratch `C:\dev\tmp\orchestrator\ingest-lead2\helper\rederive_ri214.py` (outside the repository). Done: the rule built in `_drive_sets` and used by the round-trip gate and `reconciler._check_drives`, measured on the saved corpus logs, tests with made-up records. Reader side: RI-216. **2026-11-17:** the scratch comparison named above is on the developer's laptop only and in no repository. The maintainer asks the owner for it if wanted. The rule and the done line stand without it. **2026-11-17:** two Parked notes cover the same code: RI-46 (`reconciler._check_drives` holds a per-increment record such as '4/6"-6/6"-7/6"' as a refusal, through its own '/' test) and RI-70 part (2) (the floor's `_BLOWS_OVER` has no metre unit). Read both before building.
- **RI-216** [Next] **Blow records on scanned logs: a tube sample's drive counts and the seating/test slash** (2026-11-17, build j part R, the blind set's part 1, scored by the ingest sub-lead): the model-first log reader (mf-11) misses 148 of 282 blow values on the 54 scanned logs. (1) The counts that drive a tube (U) sample are not kept as a drive record: 89 of 92 (38 sit as text in the sample's note, 51 are not in the record), though the record keeps a driven sampler's blows as a drive record of its own (`log_scoring.py:38-42`). (2) A print that writes seating and test drives as 'a,b/c,d,e,f' is kept with the slash inside one entry, and a refusal pair as two 'seating/test' strings without the printed penetrations (27 records). `SPT.blows` says a string entry is blows over a distance (`model.py:1282`), so the scorer's RI-79 rule counts them as misses (blows 160 to 134). The DIGGS writer holds such an entry as a count over a penetration in inches (RI-215); the reconciler names it in a QA note since RI-214. Done: the reader gives each count. Proposed wording (no prompt change in the release, the orchestrator's ruling of 2026-11-17; measure on the tuned set first): the `SPT.blows` description "one entry per increment, as printed; a whole count as a number. Where a log prints seating and test drives as 'a,b/c,d,e,f', the slash only separates them: give each count its own entry. A string only for a count over a distance, with the distance and its unit as printed, e.g. '25/75mm'"; and in the log brief "the blows that drive a tube or other undisturbed sampler are a driven record of their own at the sample's depth, not a note on the sample". Prompt version bump; measured on the saved corpus logs first, then on the blind set as the after score only (RI-79's rule). **2026-11-17:** the description the model-first log reader is sent is `log_reader.ReadSPT.blows` (`report_ingest/log_reader.py:214`; `model_first.LogReading` keeps today's `spt` list), not the record's `SPT` (`model.py:1282`). That field is a list of strings, so "a whole count as a number" needs either the field's type widened or the wording changed to "a whole count as its digits". **2026-11-17:** Parked RI-70 part (1) proposes a cited wording for the same field and for `ReadSPT.refusal` (FHWA NHI-06-088 Vol. I p. 3-51). ## 3. GSE on Foundry, infrastructure, releases and repo hygiene

- **INF-66** [Next] **Record the prompt-cache split on every model call, and confirm the Foundry proxy reads the cache** (build i trace review, fix 1): **code part done 2026-11-05** (merge 68adabb; cloud session, reviewed; fixes 95906a2): every `model_end` keeps `cache_read` and `cache_creation` and `turn_end` sums them, on the Claude, Foundry GPT and Funhouse Prompter routes (`module_work/inf66/CHECK.md`). Open: the live part, a Foundry turn showing calls 2-4 read the cache. each call of a short geotech turn re-sends a ~33.7k-token prefix (tools about 72 %, system about 28 %), 92.9 % of the turn's input; the cache marks go out and are accepted, but before the code part `activity.jsonl` dropped the cache-read and cache-write counts, so nobody could tell whether a Foundry turn costs about $0.23 (read) or $0.60 (not read). Done: `aec_agent_core/activity_log.py` (the core's since the app split) keeps `cache_read` and `cache_creation` in each `model_end`'s usage and sums them on `turn_end`, as report ingest does (RI-171); the next Foundry turn shows calls 2-4 reading from the cache, or the cause is found. Takes the cache split out of EV-16. *Src:* `module_work/field_feedback/2026-11-03_foundry_build_i/TRACE_REVIEW.md` §2.3 **2026-11-17:** build j (2026-11-13) carried this check; no result is recorded, so it stands on the 5.36.0 set: in one turn's `activity.jsonl` on the hosted platform, calls 2 to 4 show `cache_read` above zero. The check is the maintainer's; a fix it calls for in the model connection or the run record is in `packages/aec-agent-core`, the developer's (a core release). **2026-11-18 (the record tells a reported count from none; app `claude/b13-integration`, merge 821ffc6; built by a local helper, one independent review; not released: in the code for the release after 1.2.0):** a model call whose reply carries a cache count, zero included, is marked `cache_reported` on its `model_end`, and `turn_end` counts such calls (`cache_reported_calls`); a reply with no count writes the line it wrote before. So one turn's record now tells three cases apart: counts above zero on calls 2 to 4 (the cache is read), counts reported as zero (nothing is read), and no count reported (the proxy drops the counts, and the record cannot say). Also repaired: an empty count near the message no longer hides a real count further out in the same reply. The check on the hosted platform is still open and goes into brief 9 (EV-45), as written in `module_work/inf66/CHECK.md`; the brief says which model route it runs on, because the review noted that one route can show an all-zero cache section whatever happened.

- **INF-52** [Later] **`webapp/tests/test_public_text.py` keeps its guarded words as unsalted 16-hex SHA-256 prefixes**, so anyone can confirm a guessed word (two of the five place hashes are names already public as folder names). Use a keyed hash with the key kept out of the repo (CI secret), or a list kept local and checked only where it is present. *Src:* privacy audit 2026-10-23 **2026-11-13 (built, reviewed, not merged):** a cloud session built it on `claude/inf-52-keyed-guard` (3f53e60): the fifteen hashes stay as they are (already public in history), and words added from now on go in keyed sets (HMAC, the key from `GSE_PUBLIC_TEXT_KEY` or a file in the home folder, never the repository), which start empty. The independent review said fix first: (1) the branch was cut from 1d219c8 (2026-11-09), so merging it as it stands undoes INF-69's check of place words in tracked paths: merge master in and keep master's walk with the keyed sets added; (2) `test_empty_keyed_sets_need_no_key` asserts the committed sets are empty, so it fails when the first keyed word is committed; (3) a key file saved with a UTF-8 byte-order mark gives another key and nothing says so: read it as utf-8-sig. After the merge the owner creates the GitHub Actions secret `GSE_PUBLIC_TEXT_KEY`. It protects only words added from now on: the fifteen hashes and the old place words stay in public history (OWN-56). Left for after the downgrade: nothing needs it until a keyed word exists. **2026-11-17:** the branch is still on GitHub, now 878 commits behind master, so fix (1) is a larger merge. - **INF-16** [Later] **A kernel layer under the plotting box on Linux**: `plot_code`'s box (GM-98) is a separate process with OS limits and a Python audit hook that cannot be removed; a Python-level guard can in principle be got round by code that corrupts the interpreter's memory. On Linux, Landlock (file access, kernel 5.13+) or a network namespace would add a layer the code cannot reach. Not built because it depends on the host kernel and could look like container-escape behaviour to a host's security monitoring; measure on Funhouse and Tiny Apps first. *Src:* `packages/aec-agent-core/aec_agent_core/plot_box/child.py` (in the core since the app split), `funhouse_agent/DESIGN.md` Re-tiered 2026-11-04 (Later to Parked): GSE on Foundry has one user for a year; a kernel sandbox depends on the host and could trip its security monitoring. Revisit before any shared host. **Re-tiered 2026-11-10 (Parked to Later; deployment review):** Tiny Apps is the shared host this said to revisit before. The review confirmed the box is not a boundary there (how is kept out of this public repo), so the wrapper turns model-written plotting code off on the App Service (OWN-133) until this layer exists; one operating-system layer could also replace most of the box's four in-process ones. *Src:* `module_work/docreview_review_2026-11-10/REVIEW.md` §1.1, §3.1 **2026-11-17:** the box is in the core (`packages/aec-agent-core/aec_agent_core/plot_box/`), so this is the developer's. The 2026-11-04 reason no longer holds: the owner allows a group of about five on the hosted platform's app, sharing one conversation list (OWN-152, 2026-11-13), and model-written plotting code ran there in build j (`module_work/fde_asks/CHECK.md` section 2). The owner has ruled on it: such code is acceptable on the hosted platform, whose sandbox is tight, and stays off on the host that has connections to shared document stores (OWN-133 asks only about the testers' host). **2026-11-18 (built, off unless set; app `claude/b13-integration`, merge 452b160; built by a cloud session, one independent cloud review with three fixes; not released: in the code for the release after 1.2.0):** `GEOTECH_PLOT_CODE_KERNEL_LAYER` (unset = off; `1` = put up what the kernel offers and say what is not up; `require` = refuse the run unless the file layer is up). With it set, the box's child process puts two kernel-level layers under the Python-level guard before any plotting code runs: Landlock file rules (read only where the plotting libraries are installed, write only in the conversation's folder) and a seccomp filter that refuses socket creation and process start. Standard library only (`plot_box/kernel_layer.py`). A result says which parts are up (`box.kernel_layer`), and a probe prints what a host offers (`python -m aec_agent_core.plot_box.kernel_probe`, `--paths`). Unset, a box run and the tool's description are byte for byte what they were. **It turns nothing on by itself**: whether a host runs model-written plot code stays the owner's decision (OWN-133). **Still open:** (1) no host has run it: on each host, the probe line, the `--paths` list read by a person for anything that should not be readable, then one run at `require`; (2) the owner's decision on the filter being a deny list (`module_work/inf16/CHECK.md` section 6; it goes with OWN-133); (3) the host notes (`tinyapps/TINYAPPS.md`) do not name the setting, to be written when a host is to use it. Noted, not fixed: Landlock versions 1 to 6 and aarch64 are read, not run; fonts known only to fontconfig are not found; the process-count limit does not hold for a process run as root; add a row when Landlock version 8 appears. *Src:* `module_work/inf16/CHECK.md`, `REVIEW.md`

## 6. Document Review harness and eval suite

### Default page fixes

Testers get the page's default build (`GEOTECH_REVIEW_AGENT` unset); it took the plan's Phase-1 fixes from the lean build (DR-01 to DR-03), and the lean build itself went with DR-39.


### Review architecture (M2–M4 and the owner's questions)

- **DR-44** [Later] **The drawing-geometry layer as a fallback the agent can reach for** (owner, 2026-11-12: "I can really see it being useful on certain kinds of old as-builts and complex drawings where a conversation with a reviewer is showing that the initial path isn't working"): planlens' geometry layer (`planlens.ir`: leaders, dimensions, callouts, title blocks and revision clouds read from a sheet's vector lines) has not been on the page since DR-39 removed its switch (no suite run showed a gain, on a suite then at its ceiling). The idea: offer it as optional tools known only through their own descriptions, as `find_like` is, so the agent can turn to it when reading text and looking have not answered and the user says so; never a step of a workflow. It reads vector drawings only: a sheet plotted from CAD whose lettering is drawn as lines is its case; a scanned paper as-built has no lines to read and stays with looking and `find_like`, and each result should say which of the two a sheet is. Not before there is a test that could show a gain: a set with stroke-lettered sheets, and a task in which the user refuses the first answer (the coordination tasks are one turn each). *Src:* owner 2026-11-12 **2026-11-18 (built as an optional tool, off by default; planlens merge ea7fc9b, app merge 59d3cdb; one independent cloud review of each; not released: in the code for the release after 1.2.0 / planlens 0.16.0):** planlens offers the tool `drawing_geometry` to a host that asks (`drawing_tools=True`): on a vector sheet it lists the dimensions and leaders found in the drawn lines (ends, arrow tips, the nearest text and how far off it is). Each result says it is measured from the PDF's drawn geometry, and an empty list says none was found, with what the finders miss. On the Document Review page it comes with `GEOTECH_REVIEW_DRAWING_TOOLS=1` (in `review_flags.LATER_ENVS`) and is known only through its own description, never a step of a workflow. **Still open, as above:** there is no test yet that could show a gain (a set with stroke-lettered sheets, a task in which the user refuses the first answer), and the tool has not read a real stroke-lettered sheet (brief 9, EV-45, arm `drawing_tools`, if the owner has such a sheet to give). *Src:* `module_work/b14_wiring/CHECK.md`, `REVIEW.md`

- **DR-38** [Later] **Let the page's main model see an uploaded image itself, behind a new switch** (the old `GEOTECH_VISION_INLINE`, lean build only, went with DR-39; the inline machinery stays in the core's `aec_agent_core/deep/inline_images.py`, `aec_agent_core/inline_store.py` and the `minimal` build) (chart suite, EV-35, 2026-11-08): on Sol, Document Review as shipped scores 84 of 99 charts against 95 for the chart with a crop tool and Python, and every page loss traces to the vision side call's text (copied, re-asked, or answering a reworded question); the main model never sees the chart. Today `analyze_image` shows an image inline only for a working-folder file opened by path (`aec_agent_core/vision_tools.py`), so an attachment's first look is always a side call, switch or not. Fix: show an uploaded image to the main model inline when that switch is on. **Owner 2026-11-09: no rerun; not built until a measurement is wanted.** *Src:* `module_work/review_eval_results/2026-11-08_chart_suite_ev35/TRACE_REVIEW.md` §§2, 6 **2026-11-10 (deployment review):** the first design change to try for a stronger vision model: coordination checks (does this callout match that detail) cannot be judged from two separate descriptions written by a side call; keep side calls for bulk transcription. *Src:* `module_work/docreview_review_2026-11-10/REVIEW.md` §3.2 **2026-11-10 (built, off by default; not measured):** behind `GEOTECH_VISION_SHOW_ME=1` the page's `analyze_pdf_page`, `render_region` and `analyze_image` take `show_me`. A look with `show_me=true` runs no vision call: the image is shown to the agent's own model at its next step (the core's `deep/inline_images.py`, in the request only, the newest two views), an uploaded picture too; without it a look is today's vision call, tiles and all. The model chooses per call, and the tool descriptions say when (to judge with its own eyes; not to have a page read out). The reading helper has it as well. Off, nothing changes: the argument does not exist and the page fingerprints are untouched. Suite arm `show_me`. Left of this item: the measurement (EV-40), then the default. Each call carries the newest two images until the turn ends, so the arm's token cost is part of the result. *Src:* `packages/aec-document-review/tests/test_review_show_me_offline.py` **2026-11-13 (brief 7, EV-40):** offered in the `show_me` and all-three runs, used in none of the 70 Sol runs (no call passed `show_me=true`), so the arm measured nothing; not run again as an arm until the record counts `show_me` looks (INF-75). *Src:* `module_work/review_eval_results/2026-11-12_foundry_brief7/TRACE_REVIEW.md`, the arms **2026-11-17:** INF-75 is done (merged 2026-11-13, released in 1.1.0): the suite's RESULTS counts `show_me` looks, so the arm may run again; brief 9 (EV-45) does not carry it. Left: a `show_me` run, read for whether any call used it, then the default. Keeper: split: the maintainer runs it on the hosted platform; the developer reads the record and sets the default. **2026-11-18 (a second way built, off by default; app `claude/b13-integration`, merge ca96205; built by a local helper, one independent cloud review with two fixes; not released: in the code for the release after 1.2.0):** behind `GEOTECH_VISION_SHOW_UPLOADS` (in `review_flags.LATER_ENVS`) the page's main model looking at an image file the user uploaded is handed the image itself at its next step, with no choice to make and no vision call (the 2026-11-10 `show_me` argument was offered and never used). One shown image is one image call on the turn's count and ceiling. A PDF, a file opened by its path, a zoom and every helper's look stay as they are; an image too large to show is read by a vision call as before and its result says so; an upload left out of a batch of looks (a model call carries the newest two images) is named as not shown. The record says whether the page shows uploads and why (`build.show_uploads`). Unset, the page is byte for byte what it was. **Set it only where the main model takes images**: the page takes a model with no profile to accept them, and a text-only model's turn then fails at the first call after a look. A probe at build (`inline_images.probe(model)`) or a fallback on the first refusal is not built. Noted, not fixed: a look left out of a batch still counts an image call; a second look at one upload stores a second copy and counts again; the inline store holds up to 96 images of up to 4 MB each on a shared host; the suite's `show_me_looks` column does not count a shown upload. Left: the measurement (brief 9, EV-45, arm `show_uploads`; one probe per model route first), then the default. *Src:* `module_work/dr38/CHECK.md`, `REVIEW.md`

### Markups and checks

- **DR-30** [Parked] **The markup check returns a corrected box for rings and rectangles only**; callout, note and highlight looks are not asked for one (asking would change how their area check is measured). Also: the correction count runs per marked copy, not per turn, so a later "try again" meets the stop note until a clean check resets it. Live (F03): two boxes cut the note they mark but passed, because the check tests the note's centre; when a box is confirmed but cuts the thing, return the box the look found. **Parked 2026-10-27** (likely too narrow to implement, under the owner's rule against chasing edge cases): Hardens the markup check for more look types and a case seen once (F03); better models likely make it moot.

The first model run is EV-40. *Src:* `module_work/review_eval_results/2026-11-10_coordination_tasks_blind/README.md` **2026-11-17:** the coordination tasks have run on models: brief 7 (EV-40) and brief 8 (EV-43, three repeats). Left as above: a UFGS specification section, a real long set (the owner's documents, private), and the orientation turn in a run. Keeper: split: the developer builds tasks; the maintainer runs them on the hosted platform. **2026-11-18 (a specification section with a submittal, and a reissue of the set; merge 5e45b24; built by a local helper, one independent review and a cloud recheck, every must-fix and should-fix finding fixed; not released: in the code for the release after 1.2.0):** two tasks on the made-up building, 54 in all: a window specification section in three parts with its submittal (a complying value given beside the governing one, a reference to a section that does not exist, a specified test the report leaves out, a schedule of values whose total is not its sum), and a reissue of the drawing set (a change made on one sheet and not on its twin, a revision cloud with no entry in the revision block, an entry with nothing changed, a schedule row renumbered while the plan's tag was not). Nine checks, one per planted problem, and each reads ONE finding at a time (the core's `stated_together`, `scope="finding"`: a list item, a table row, a line), so terms that stand anywhere in a reply do not pass; scores on these tasks are not comparable with a run before this. Not run on a model: brief 9 (EV-45). **Known limits of the nine checks, left as built (the lead's decision; they are why the traces of these tasks are read in full):** a finding stated and then taken back in the same paragraph passes; each plain line is its own finding, so a finding split over label lines under a heading or a hard-wrapped paragraph can fail; two numbers side by side pass the two value checks whatever the verdict; the right words about another thing on the same line pass. **Left of this item:** a real long set (the owner's documents, private), and the orientation turn in a run (the check notes say what turning it on costs and would measure; the default is unchanged). *Src:* `module_work/dr25/CHECK.md`, `REVIEW.md`

## 7. Vision

- **VIS-02** [Parked] **`found` B1 / B2**: judge region vs text line by aspect; carry the text read (`vision_view.py:775, 804`). *was:* H E3 **Parked 2026-10-27** (likely too narrow to implement, under the owner's rule against chasing edge cases): Vision heuristic tuning (region versus text line by aspect); a better model likely makes it moot.
- **VIS-30** [Later] **E-size sheets: tiles still under the legibility line (the note saying so is kept since aec-agent-core 1.1.0)** (deployment review 2026-11-10): on a 36 x 48 in sheet a 4 x 4 tile shows 4.3 pt lettering at about 9 px, under the app's own 12 px line (`planlens/review/frames.py`), and the result's `legibility` note is removed once tiles were read (`vision_tools._read_pdf_page`), so dimension strings and keynotes count as read. Keep the note when tile lettering is still under the line; tile by the sheet's own regions (title block, general notes, schedules, legend, each view) with a second level where needed, since a uniform grid cuts dimension strings and revision clouds at tile edges. By arithmetic; the misread rate is not measured. **2026-11-13 (the note is kept; master 6b92058, with VIS-31 part 1):** a tiled page read keeps the `legibility` note while any tile's lettering is under the line, names those tiles with whole-number pixel heights, and says so where the height is the assumed lettering size because the page has no text to measure (`vision_tools._tile_legibility`); the note goes only when every tile reads. Noted, not fixed: an agent that is not offered `render_region` (the geotech page's calc helper) still reads "zoom with render_region" in the note, because `deep/vision_kit._without_zoom_tool` strips `zoom_hint` and not `legibility`. Open: tiling by the sheet's own regions, which needs a design and a measurement on E-size sets. *Src:* `module_work/docreview_review_2026-11-10/REVIEW.md` §2.5, §3.2 **2026-11-17:** the kept note is released in aec-agent-core 1.1.0 (app 5.36.0). What is left is the region tiling. Today the page reader cuts a uniform n x n grid (`tile_boxes` in planlens `planlens/review/frames.py`; the count from `_tile_count` in `packages/aec-agent-core/aec_agent_core/vision_tools.py`). Cut the tiles along the sheet's own regions instead, with a second level where a region's lettering is still under `LEGIBLE_TEXT_PX` (12 px); planlens 0.16.0's sheet index (`planlens/document/sheets.py`) already finds where the title block's numbers sit across sheets, a possible start. Done when, on an E-size made-up set, each region's lettering reads at 12 px or more, no dimension string or revision cloud is cut at a tile edge, and the `legibility` note goes; measured against today's grid on the same sheets (a model run: on the hosted platform, on a brief). Neither repository has an E-size set (planlens' drawing fixtures are 24 x 36 in), so one is built first. The noted case's code is `_without_zoom_tool` in `packages/aec-agent-core/aec_agent_core/deep/vision_kit.py`.

Two tools, `sheet_index` and `check_cross_references`, for a host that asks (`ReviewHost(toolkit_options={"sheet_tools": True})`); every existing tool list and result is unchanged. An independent review ran in three passes before the merge; its rule is in the code: a callout is `unresolved` only where the sheet is in the file and no mark for that view was recognised on it, `not_in_document` where no sheet of that number was read (a finding only if the file is the whole set, which the agent judges from the cover's index), and `cannot_tell` wherever the text does not settle it. ## 10. Tiny Apps, Funhouse and the app split

- **Rollout model (owner, 2026-10-12):** testers get ONE deployment, GPT-5.6 Sol or higher, for every call; not the pilot's `tinyapp-gpt-medium` (GPT-5.1, 768 px). The app measures whatever answers on first use (`funhouse_agent/vision_probe.py`), and planlens maps `gpt-5.6` to `openai-high` / `openai-original`. Sol has run the suite at full resolution on Foundry (5.32.0rc3; B5 39/42).
