### The app split (Document Review leaves this repo)

- **INF-26** [Later] **Split Document Review from GeotechStaffEngineer** (owner 2026-10-20): Document Review becomes its own package (on PyPI as `aec-document-review` since 2026-11-10; the owner chose PyPI on 2026-11-09, INF-33), installed in Tiny Apps; GeotechStaffEngineer runs as its own app in Palantir Foundry. **Plan merged 2026-10-20** (`module_work/APP_SPLIT_PLAN.md`, commit `aaf93ef`): three packages of our own from this repository (working names: `de-docreview`, the Document Review app, marked so PyPI refuses it; `docreview-core`, what both apps run, public on PyPI like planlens; GSE on PyPI as today); 16 steps after a step 0, each merged and tested on its own, guarded by a test that builds the Document Review agent with every geotech package blocked and by fingerprints of both pages' agents. Decided: the geotech page leaves Tiny Apps (owner, desk 2026-10-20 08:33); the testers' agent moves unchanged, `lean` decided later by DR-05 (lead, the owner can overrule). Owner decisions: OWN-72 (names, Tiny Apps route, CfA questions 6a-6d), OWN-74 (shared code), OWN-75 (release first), OWN-76 (public or private), OWN-77 (figure helper and plotting code), OWN-78 (notice and SharePoint folder). Steps: INF-30 to INF-35. *Src:* owner 2026-10-20 **Owner 2026-10-20 (after the first Foundry run):** Document Review is Tiny Apps only; Foundry only if Tiny Apps fails after its six-month pilot, and not planned around. So the split stands: two hosts with different rules (Foundry: Claude, no SharePoint, its own download and storage limits; Tiny Apps: GPT, SharePoint, CfA's pins). Order (lead, same day): the host fixes from the Foundry run (INF-36 to INF-39) go into today's package, into the shell that step 10 moves to the core; steps 3-8 (INF-30, INF-31) go on; steps 9-16 (INF-32 to INF-35) wait until the Tiny Apps tester start is set and the Foundry wheel route is known (OWN-80), as each new package adds a release. **2026-10-21 (INF-48):** plan rewritten for OWN-74 and OWN-76. Document Review becomes private source in the GHE wrapper repo (no package, no wheel; superseded 2026-11-09, a PyPI package, INF-33). A thin public core goes on PyPI (about 37,900 lines; the owner's three named parts about 21,100). The document tools that only wrap planlens move into planlens (PL-29). Each app keeps its own chat page (about 12,900 lines, copied once at step 13). Steps 1-8 stand (step 7 now prepares the copy); steps 9-16 are rewritten: 9 planlens (PL-29), 10-11 the core (INF-32), 12-13 its release and Document Review's private home (INF-33), 14 the wrapper (INF-34), 15-16 GSE lets go and notes (INF-35). Still gated: OWN-72 (the name before step 10; CfA's 6a before step 14), OWN-78 (before step 14), OWN-87 (before step 13). **2026-11-17:** steps 1 to 14 are merged and released: the core and Document Review at 1.1.0 under one tag `aec-vX.Y.Z` (INF-76), planlens 0.16.0, the app 5.36.0 pinning `aec-agent-core==1.1.0`. The gates in the sentence above are settled (OWN-72 merged into OWN-44; OWN-78 and OWN-87 answered, folded into INF-34 and INF-33). Open: step 14's exit (INF-34) and steps 15-16 (INF-35). The packages and Document Review are the developer's; the GSE side of steps 15-16 changes the app, which is the maintainer's from 5.36.0 (INF-35).
- **INF-34** [Later] **App split, step 14 (the Tiny Apps wrapper)** (INF-26 §4): the GHE wrapper repo installs `aec-document-review` from Nexus by `packages.txt` (owner 2026-11-09, INF-33; else one wheel of ours installed by path; the source-folder route stays in the template); `packages.txt` pins the core and planlens from Nexus and restores pandas==3.0.2; the notice as proposed and testers' conversations kept in `Shared Documents/GeotechStaffEngineer` (owner 2026-10-21); exit: the owner's round on the test machine (OWN-29, rewritten). *Src:* INF-26 plan, rewritten 2026-10-21 (INF-48) **2026-11-04, ready for any route (merge a894ce6, review fix 7a4d782):** the wrapper template's `run.sh` takes our code from `app_src/` (on `PYTHONPATH`, with an optional `app_src/requirements.txt`), from `wheels/*.whl`, or from Nexus by `packages.txt`, picked by `APP_SOURCE` or else by what the repo holds; CfA's fleet pins moved to `requirements-thirdparty.txt`, which `packages.txt` includes, so the Nexus route installs exactly what it did; `APP_DRY_RUN=1` prints the route and commands; `run.sh` kept LF by `.gitattributes`; tests `webapp/tests/test_tinyapps_wrapper.py`. So CfA's answer to 6a-6d is a file drop on dosdev. Noted, not fixed: routes A and B do not read `packages.txt`, which CfA's guide calls the install list (ask with 6a-6d whether their deploy reads it); nothing builds `app_src/` yet (step 13). **Step 14 merged 2026-11-10 (47db5a3; cloud session, reviewed, its diff put onto master as b181798 with fixes 82a9eb9 to 486037b):** the template installs `aec-document-review==0.1.0` with the core and planlens pinned exactly and pandas==3.0.2; `app.py` imports `aec_document_review.tinyapps_entry`; a `.gitignore` keeps `.env` and `.venv` out of the wrapper repo; `tinyapps/TINYAPPS.md` is for Document Review alone, with the owner's numbered first-round checklist and the platform team's asks (ask 4 names the shared and the owner's own model key); the wheels route installs and serves the page end to end (`module_work/inf34/CHECK_STEP14.md`). **Open:** the owner's round on the test machine (OWN-30, which took in OWN-29), once the three packages reach Nexus. **2026-11-17:** what is left: the owner's pin bump in the testers' wrapper once OWN-147 is answered, and the live check on it, TA-42. The owner accepts that for a group of about five. Separating viewers needs Foundry to pass the viewer's identity into the app: a question for the FDE and the platform, then the page's existing per-person folders (as on Tiny Apps). *Src:* brief 8's hand-back, 2026-11-13, part A **2026-11-17:** split: the question to the platform and the check on the hosted platform are the maintainer's, with the geotech page's `webapp/identity.py`; Document Review's per-person folders there are the developer's (`packages/aec-document-review/aec_document_review/identity.py`).

Probes that give them would let INF-77's sweep find a dead one. *Src:* `module_work/inf77/CHECK.md` **2026-11-17:** the maintainer's (the app's standing tests, `funhouse_agent/tests/`), though it sits in section 10.
- **INF-84** [Next] **The step-cap answer's first live turn** (2026-11-17): `GEOTECH_STEP_CAP_ANSWER` is on in the app since 5.36.0 (merge 03ae5ce; `webapp/core.py`, `STEP_CAP_APP_DEFAULT`) (a turn that reaches the step cap answers from its work so far, in one call with no tools, with the plain message and the setting to raise under it; `0` turns it off); it has run only with fake models. On the first real turn that reaches the cap, read the answer and the call's tokens in the activity log. **2026-11-17:** one of the maintainer's first live checks on the hosted platform (the geotech page), though it sits in section 10. On the same host, read also the first turn that reaches a figure cap of the same merge (one figure at most 3 draws and 3 looks a turn, 10 draws a helper call; `module_work/fde_asks/CHECK.md` section 2): the turn answers with the figure it has.
- **INF-78** [Later] **A Google model (Gemini) on Foundry: a third engine family** (2026-11-13, OWN-151; from brief 8's hand-back, 2026-11-13, part B): the FDE found that a Gemini model is reached from an app container only through the language-model proxy's Google door (`.../proxy/google/v1/models/<model>:generateContent`, the container's token), in Google's own request and response shape: not OpenAI-compatible, and `palantir_models` has no Google class. Images go as inline data; every image costs a fixed 1,089 tokens whatever its size (256 at low resolution), so small print on a whole sheet is lost unless the page is cropped or tiled first. Tools work, and an image returned by a tool must sit inside the function response's own parts; the model's thought signatures must be sent back with its turn; reasoning is set by a thinking level, not a budget; token counts come back, with no rate-limit headers and no token-counting call; a request may be up to 50 MB. What a build needs: a chat model class for that shape in the core (`engine_config.py` knows `anthropic` and `openai` on Foundry today), the vision engine's image budget for a fixed-size model, and a standing-test run before anyone uses it. *Src:* brief 8's hand-back, 2026-11-13, REPORT_B (private) **2026-11-17:** split: the chat model class and the vision engine's image budget are in the core (`packages/aec-agent-core`), the developer's; the question to the platform and the standing-test run on the hosted platform are the maintainer's.
- **INF-35** [Later] **App split, steps 15-16 (GSE lets go; notes)** (INF-26 §4): 15, after step 14's exit or the owner's word: the frozen review files, the review profile, GSE's Tiny Apps entry and the two-page default leave this repository; the reverse boundary test (GSE builds with no review file present); a GSE release; 16, one release later: the import aliases out; every doc, notebook cell, Harness Map entry and helper prompt swept to the new paths; BACKLOG items tagged with their home (planlens, the core, GSE, Document Review). **For step 16 (from step 11 (a)'s review):** `funhouse_agent/chart_eval/runner.py` finds the engine module by its old name, so it changes with the callers when the aliases go. **Also for step 16 (doc review, 2026-11-10):** planlens' `planlens/review/tests/capture_app_specs.py` imports `funhouse_agent.deep.vision_kit`, an alias; it should import `aec_agent_core.deep.vision_kit` before the aliases go, or a re-capture fails. *Src:* INF-26 plan, rewritten 2026-10-21 (INF-48) **2026-11-17 (the orchestrator's call, for the owner to revisit):** since the hand-over the GSE side of steps 15 and 16 (the frozen review files, the review profile, GSE's Tiny Apps entry and the two-page default out of GSE; the reverse boundary test; a GSE release; the import aliases out of GSE's callers) changes the app: it waits for the renewal of this repository (`MAINTAINERS.md` section 9); the maintainer may do it sooner in its own copy, and nothing waits on it. The developer's part is planlens' `planlens/review/tests/capture_app_specs.py` (import `aec_agent_core.deep.vision_kit`, before the aliases go) and the core's side of the aliases; INF-71's single binding of planlens' tools is in the core, the developer's.

## Done since the last sweep

Older docs still list these as open. Items closed before the last sweep are in `BACKLOG_DONE.md`, newest sweep first.

- **VIS-26** 2026-11-18 (app merge 1193f28, fingerprint 1f10f8c; planlens merge 803e591; built by a local helper, one independent review, its three should-fix findings fixed; not released: in the code for the release after 1.2.0 / planlens 0.16.0): **an image file's positions are its pixels everywhere**. `measure` on an image takes its boxes in the image's pixels and gives every box in its result back in them (a key that ends in `_pt` stays in the page's points, and `frame` says so with the factor); `log_grid` says in `frame` how its positions read; `kept_readings` lists an image zoom with a `frame`; the minimal page's prompt and `page_count` say what the tools do with a picture, and `page_count` on an image returns one page, not an error (this moved `review_minimal`'s fingerprint; the default build is unchanged). In planlens: a mark anchored on an image view is judged by the scale the view was sent at (placed when sent at full scale or larger, refused when sent shrunk, in words about pixels), and with append on, a marked copy that holds another image is passed over for a new copy under the next free name. Each is a repair, on, no switch; a PDF's results are byte for byte as before. The marks part reaches a host only with the planlens release after 0.16.0 (the core hands planlens the sent scale only when the installed planlens takes it). Not run on the testers' model: the live check of a photograph with an EXIF turn and of `find_like` on a pasted screenshot, which stays in `module_work/LIVE_TEST_QUEUE.md` (check 9's extension). *Src:* `module_work/vis26/CHECK.md`
- **TA-46** 2026-11-18 (merges b6daee3 and 74615f3; built by a local helper, one independent cloud review with eight fixes, its three open findings built by a second cloud session and read by the lead; not released: in the code for the release after 1.2.0): **the run record, and the error text a tool hands the model, mask a secret an error names** (found on 2026-11-18; never an open item). The handlers for a failed tool and a failed model call wrote the exception's text into `activity.jsonl` as it came, and a tool's error line went to the model the same way; an exception from a model client or an HTTP library can carry a key, a bearer token, a cookie or a signed URL, and the record is what a tester downloads and sends on. Now `error_text.mask_error_secrets` masks a secret by its form or its place (named keys and settings, bearer and cookie headers, signed and percent-encoded URLs, a private-key block) and keeps an unlabelled id or hash, so a request id still traces a failure; `mask_secrets` is unchanged for the places that use it. Masked: the record's error fields, the turn's `error_detail`, error text kept one level down in the record (the vision probe's error, the photograph-page check's `why`, the instructions folder's errors), the review suite's result (a raised check's detail; an older saved result is masked when read back), what `error_line` and `tool_error` hand the model, and three plain messages the model reads (a SharePoint upload error, the minimal page's "The helper failed", "Feedback could not be recorded"). **A repair, on, no switch (the lead's ruling):** an error with no secret reads character for character as before, and no prompt, tool description or fingerprint moved. A judgment, not a measurement: outside a URL query the bare names `key`, `token`, `secret`, `signature`, `sig` and `code` are masked only where the value is 16 or more characters with a letter and a digit. Not covered (noted, left): a value split by a line break, Digest and `Pwd=` forms, a URL encoded twice, and the server's own console lines. The geotech page's own transcript and the turn error it shows are written in `webapp/`, the maintainer's, and are not masked by this. *Src:* `module_work/record_masks/CHECK.md`, `REVIEW.md`, `FIX.md`
- **TA-45** 2026-11-18 (merges 026d351 and 3403e27; built by a local helper, one independent cloud review, its first finding and four notes built by a second cloud session and read by the lead; not released: in the code for the release after 1.2.0): **a failed summary no longer replaces a conversation's older history, and is not tried again in the same turn** (found while sorting TA-24; never an open item). The agent library's own summarizer shortens a long conversation with a model call of its own. When that call failed, langchain 1.3.14 and older put the line "Error generating summary: ..." in place of the older history and the turn went on without it; 1.3.15 and newer ended the turn on the provider's error. Now (`aec_agent_core/deep/summary_guard.py`): the model is asked again with the history whole, the record gains a `summary_failed` event, and the later model calls of that turn are handed the history as it is, with no summary request, back-off or new history file (`summaries_skipped` on `turn_end` counts them); the next turn tries again. **No switch. On the older library it is a repair; on the current one it changes what a turn does (the lead's decision):** before, the turn ended on the provider's error; now it goes on with the whole history, and where that is too long for the model the library summarizes regardless or the call ends on the error. The guard installs under a lock, and where the library's shape is unknown it is absent and says so once in the log. Noted, not fixed: each failing turn still leaves one copy of the older history in the conversation's history files. Not checked on a real provider or a page turn: brief 9 (EV-45) counts `summary_failed` and `summaries_skipped` in every run; zero is the expected number. *Src:* `module_work/b14_ta/CHECK.md`, `REVIEW.md`, `FIX.md`
- **EV-49** 2026-11-18 (merge 5e45b24; built by a local helper, one independent cloud review, which found it holds as built; not released: in the code for the release after 1.2.0): `review_flags.LATER_SETTINGS_ENVS` names `GEOTECH_TURN_MAX_IMAGE_CALLS`; `switches()` clears it unless the arm names it, and `describe()` says its value, so the turn's `build.switches` shows it. An arm that does not name the setting runs with no ceiling though the environment sets one (`packages/aec-agent-core/tests/test_ev49_image_ceiling_between_arms.py`). `SETTINGS_ENVS`, `ALL_ENVS` and `ARMS` are unchanged, so the geotech copy's pinned test is not touched; DR-51 folds the new name in with the switches. On 1.2.0 itself the ceiling still carries between arms: brief 9's first parts say so. *Src:* `module_work/ev49/CHECK.md`

- **PL-41** 2026-11-18 (planlens merge e1a33dd; built by a local helper, one independent review, nothing to fix; not released: in the code for the release after planlens 0.16.0): where the sheet index has been made for a document (only a host that asks for the sheet tools makes it), each page the index numbered from its title block shows that number on the page map, and the page's own reading is kept beside it (`sheet_read_alone`) where the two differ. - **PL-43** 2026-11-18 (planlens merge 2b12147; built by a local helper, one independent review, its two findings fixed by a second helper; not released: in the code for the release after planlens 0.16.0): three limits settled and one narrowed; all of it runs only for a host that asks for the sheet tools. (1) A sheet whose drawing is not read (an image, a scan, lettering that is not text) no longer lets a mark on it be called missing from the schedule, or a row be called drawn nowhere: such items are held open and the sheet is named. (2) A sheet showing fewer than two of a schedule's plain-number marks borrows the lettering those marks have elsewhere; what it finds is never more than `cannot_tell`. (3) Where a schedule's rows are read only from the hidden text a CAD program keeps with lettering drawn as lines, a row not found is `cannot_tell` only where a sheet that could show it carries none of that text; where every such sheet carries it, the row stays `no_mark` as in 0.16.0.
